### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 11:31:48 +0200

Ran both. Two constraints bit, and I let them bite:

```
$ git tag -a 'BITER ATTACK!!' -m 'test'
fatal: 'BITER ATTACK!!' is not a valid tag name.
exit code: 128
```

```
$ git tag -a 'BITER_ATTACK!!' -m 'run 2 died'
fatal: tag 'BITER_ATTACK!!' already exists
exit code: 128
```

Repo is back to zero tags — the test tag was created and deleted, nothing persisted.

## What actually failed

**1. Spaces are illegal in git ref names.** Not a style preference — git refs are paths on disk and in the packed-refs file, so the forbidden set is hard: space, `~`, `^`, `:`, `?`, `*`, `[`, `\`. Your `!!` is completely legal and survives intact. Only the spaces have to go.

**2. Tag names are unique per repo — this is the real problem.** Run 2's biter attack cannot be called what run 1's was called. Since the entire point is that this happens *many times*, a bare name can never work. Every tag needs a discriminator.

That second one is worth sitting with, because it's not a git quirk you work around — it's git telling you something true about your design. **A named coordinate has to say *which* reset, or it isn't a coordinate.** The naming scheme is forced to carry the run identity whether you wanted it to or not.

## The decision I can't make for you

The discriminator is yours. But there's an ambiguity buried in it that you should resolve deliberately, because it'll be wrong forever if it gets picked by accident:

**Does the number belong to the run that died, or the run that's born?**

```
BITER_ATTACK_003!!            ← run 3 dies
SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY_004   ← run 4 begins
```
Unambiguous, but the pair sits adjacent in the log with mismatched numbers, and every future query means remembering the offset.

```
BITER_ATTACK_003!!            ← ends run 3
SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY_003   ← ...also 003?
```
Reads as a matched pair, but now "003" means two different things depending on which tag it's on.

There's a third option — date instead of number (`BITER_ATTACK_2026-08-18!!`), which sorts chronologically, never collides, and dodges the question entirely. Costs you the ability to say "run 3" out loud.

## Two other things this surfaced

**Run 001 can't be "SAME SHIP DIFFERENT DAY."** It's the first day. And you already have a commit called `Crash` (`234770e`) sitting right where that tag would go — the arrival is already in the history, named. So either run 001's origin tag is a one-off, or `Crash` gets tagged and the pattern starts at run 002.

**Where does `BITER_ATTACK` land — on the death, or before it?** I'd put it on the scuttle commit itself, the one whose entire diff is the deletion. Then `git show BITER_ATTACK_003!!` shows you the corpse, file by file, and `git log SAME_SHIP..BITER_ATTACK` gives you the run's whole story with its death as the last line. Two tags per boundary, no third needed.

Give me the discriminator and whether the number tracks the dead run or the new one, and I'll write the ritual up properly and plant the first coordinate.

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 11:41:22 +0200

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 11:41:36 +0200

Direct answer: **no, not by default — but the date is always stored, and it's one flag away. Date-in-the-name is redundant.**

```
$ git tag -l                          ← what you get by default
BITER_ATTACK_10!!
BITER_ATTACK_2026-08-17!!
BITER_ATTACK_3!!                      ← no dates at all
```

```
$ git tag -l --format='...'           ← asking for them
BITER_ATTACK_10!!   tagged:2026-08-18  commit:2026-08-17  Session 003: we forgot the guy...
```

And `git show BITER_ATTACK_3!!` gives you tagger, date, your message, the commit it points at, *and* the diffstat of what died. GitHub's tags page shows dates next to every tag without you doing anything.

So the date is never lost. But three things did bite in that test:

## 1. The tag date is when you *tagged*, not when the run happened

Every test tag read `tagged: 2026-08-18` while pointing at commits from `2026-08-17`. If you ever tag a run a day late — or retro-tag — the tag's own date lies about the run. The commit date is the truth. Ask for `%(*committerdate)` (the star means "the commit this tag points at"), not `%(creatordate)`.

## 2. Numbers sort alphabetically, and it's already broken

```
BITER_ATTACK_10!!     ← run 10
BITER_ATTACK_3!!      ← run 3, listed second
```

Run 10 sorted before run 3. Two fixes: `--sort=version:refname`, or **zero-pad to three digits** (`003`, `010`). Pad it — the flag only works when someone remembers to type it, and GitHub's tag page won't. Padding works everywhere, forever.

## 3. Mixed schemes fight each other

`BITER_ATTACK_2026-08-17!!` sorted after both numbered tags. If you ever use both forms, the list interleaves nonsensically. Pick one.

## The rule I'd take from this

**Put in the name only what git cannot tell you.**

Git already knows both dates. Git has no idea what run number this is — that fact exists nowhere except in your head and the Ship's ledger. So the number goes in the name and the date stays out of it:

```
BITER_ATTACK_003!!
SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY_004
```

The one honest argument for dates in the name is if you ever lose count of the runs. The Ship's ledger is supposed to be the thing that prevents that — and if it can't, that's a ledger failure worth finding out about rather than papering over with filenames.

## The navigation surface you were actually asking about

The bare listing is useless and the useful one is unmemorable. Fix that once with an alias, so the whole ledger is one word:

```bash
git config alias.runs "tag -l --sort=version:refname \
  --format='%(refname:short)%09%(*committerdate:short)%09%(subject)'"
```

Then `git runs` gives you every reset boundary, in order, with the real commit date and your message — the ledger's spine as a single command. Want me to set that alias up?

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 11:49:36 +0200

Both tested. Repo is back to zero tags.

## Yes to multiple tags per commit — unlimited

```
$ git tag --points-at 70e0e7b
PROJECT_START
RUN_002
SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY_002
```

Three tags, one commit, no complaint. That part of your scheme works exactly as you described.

## But `RUN_002` solo and `RUN_002/...` cannot coexist

```
$ git tag -a 'RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!' -m 'run 2 dies' f007148
fatal: cannot lock ref 'refs/tags/RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!':
       'refs/tags/RUN_002' exists; cannot create 'refs/tags/RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!'
```

Refs are literally files on disk under `.git/refs/tags/`. `RUN_002` can be a **file** or a **directory**. Never both. The moment `RUN_002` exists as a tag, nothing can live underneath it.

Delete the solo tag and the slash form works immediately — that's the whole fix.

## And the deeper one: a tag can't span a range

"RUN_002 (solo) = the whole 2nd run" isn't something a tag is able to do, even without the file/directory clash. **A tag is a pin at one commit.** A run is the *span between two pins*. That's not a limitation to work around — it's the correct model, because a run genuinely is a range and git has first-class syntax for ranges.

## Your scheme, with the one adjustment

Drop solo `RUN_002`. Keep `RUN_002` as a **prefix**, which gives you the grouping you were reaching for:

```
PROJECT_START                          ← one-time, top level
RUN_001/BITER_ATTACK!!
RUN_002/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY
RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!
RUN_003/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY
RUN_003/BITER_ATTACK!!
```

And it does everything you asked for:

| You want | Command |
|---|---|
| Everything in run 2 | `git tag -l 'RUN_002/*'` |
| The whole 2nd run | `git log RUN_002/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY..RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!` |
| What run 2 built | `git diff RUN_002/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!! --stat` |
| Exactly what died | `git show RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!` |
| What The Ship carried across | `git diff RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!! RUN_003/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY` |

GitHub also groups slash-namespaced tags in its tag list, so the run structure is visible to a visitor without them running anything.

`!!` survives inside the namespace fine — confirmed in the test.

## Two things to decide

**1. Run 001 is asymmetric.** It has no `SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY` — it's the first day, and `PROJECT_START` is its opening pin. So run 1's range is `PROJECT_START..RUN_001/BITER_ATTACK!!` while every later run is `RUN_00N/SAME_SHIP..RUN_00N/BITER_ATTACK!!`. You can either accept a one-off asymmetry, or put `RUN_001/PROJECT_START` inside the namespace so one glob pattern covers every run forever. I'd take the second — it costs nothing now and saves a special case in every query you ever write.

**2. Does the number on `BITER_ATTACK` mean the run that died?** Under the prefix scheme this resolves itself cleanly: `RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!` unambiguously means "the death of run 2," and `RUN_003/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY` means "the birth of run 3." The earlier ambiguity disappears because the namespace carries the ownership. That's a real argument for the prefix beyond just grouping.

## One thing that will bite later

**Tags don't push by default.** `git push` sends commits and leaves tags on your machine. A ledger that never reaches GitHub isn't public. You'll need `git push origin --tags`, or push each tag as you create it. Worth building into the reset ritual rather than discovering after run 3.

Confirm the two decisions and I'll write the ritual up, set the `git runs` alias, and plant `PROJECT_START`.

Read before scuttling a run.\n\n---\n\n## What this solves\n\nGit never deletes anything. A reset destroys the working tree, never the\nhistory — every file the planet ever held stays in the commits that held it,\npermanently.\n\nSo preservation is free and is not the problem. The problem is **navigation**:\nmaking run boundaries findable without reading the whole log. This document is\nthe coordinate system.\n\n**The Ship is the durable ledger. The planet is disposable.**\n\n---\n\n## The coordinate scheme\n\nAnnotated tags, namespaced per run, numbers zero-padded to three digits.\n\n```\nRUN_001/PROJECT_START\nRUN_001/BITER_ATTACK!!\nRUN_002/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY\nRUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!\nRUN_003/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY\n```\n\nEvery rule below was forced by git, not chosen:\n\n| Rule | Why |\n|---|---|\n| No spaces in tag names | `fatal: 'BITER ATTACK!!' is not a valid tag name` — refs are paths on disk |\n| Zero-pad to three digits | Tags sort alphabetically. `RUN_10` sorts *before* `RUN_3` |\n| `RUN_00N` is a prefix, never a tag on its own | `fatal: cannot lock ref` — a ref can be a file or a directory, never both |\n| The number is the run that **died** | `RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!` ends run 2. `RUN_003/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY` begins run 3 |\n| No date in the name | Git already stores the tag date *and* the commit date. The run number exists nowhere else |\n\n`!!` is legal in a git ref. Only spaces had to go.\n\n### A run is a range, not a tag\n\nA tag pins one commit. A run is the span between two pins. This is not a\nlimitation to work around — a run genuinely is a range, and git has first-class\nsyntax for ranges.\n\n`RUN_001` is asymmetric by design: it opens on `PROJECT_START` because there was\nno previous ship to be the same ship as. Every run after it opens on\n`SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY`.\n\n---\n\n## The ritual\n\nThree commits, two tags, one push. Do not improvise the order.\n\n**1. The toll.** File the entry naming what the world failed at. Lands in The\nShip. A reset is legal only after this entry exists — pay the toll, then burn\nit. The reset produces the resource rather than spending it.\n\n**2. The scuttle.** One commit. **Pure deletion, nothing else in it.** If a\ndeletion is mixed with additions the diff becomes unreadable, and being readable\nis this commit's entire job. The commit message is the epitaph.\n\n```bash\ngit rm -r --cached <planet paths>   # stage the removals\ngit commit                          # message = what the world failed at\ngit tag -a 'RUN_00N/BITER_ATTACK!!' -m '<what died and why>'\n```\n\n**3. The landing.** Scaffold the new world.\n\n```bash\ngit commit\ngit tag -a 'RUN_00M/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY' -m '<what carried across>'\n```\n\n**4. Push the tags.** `git push` sends commits and **leaves tags behind.** A\nledger that never reaches GitHub is not public.\n\n```bash\ngit push origin --tags\n```\n\n### The invariant\n\n**A scuttle commit must delete nothing under `The Ship/`.** This is mechanically\ncheckable and should become a hook rather than a promise.\n\n---\n\n## Navigation\n\n| Question | Command |\n|---|---|\n| Every boundary, in order | `git runs` |\n| Everything tagged in run 2 | `git tag -l 'RUN_002/*'` |\n| The whole story of run 2 | `git log RUN_002/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY..RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!` |\n| What run 2 built | `git diff RUN_002/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!! --stat` |\n| Exactly what died | `git show RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!` |\n| What The Ship carried across | `git diff RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!! RUN_003/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY` |\n| The repo as it stood at a boundary | Click the tag on GitHub |\n\nRanges exclude their left endpoint. To include the opening commit itself, add a\ncaret:\n\n```bash\ngit log RUN_001/PROJECT_START^..RUN_001/BITER_ATTACK!!\n```\n\n### The measurement this gives away for free\n\n`git diff RUN_00N/BITER_ATTACK!! RUN_00M/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY` is the\nreset-cost instrument. A scuttle diff that **shrinks run over run** is the\nontology doing its job. One that stays the same size is the falsification.\n\nNo instrument had to be built. It only required planting the tags.\n\n---\n\n## The `git runs` alias\n\nLives in `.git/config`, which is **not committed** — it does not travel to\nanyone who clones this repo. Recreate it with:\n\n```bash\ngit config alias.runs \"tag -l --sort=version:refname \\\n  --format='%(refname:short)%09%(*committerdate:short)%09%(subject)'\"\n```\n\n`%(*committerdate)` is the date of the *commit the tag points at*, not the date\nthe tag was created. Those differ whenever a tag is planted late, and the commit\ndate is the one that tells the truth about the run.\n\n---\n\n## Current state\n\n| Tag | Commit | Meaning |\n|---|---|---|\n| `RUN_001/PROJECT_START` | `24efdd4` | First commit whose root CLAUDE.md carries no Business motives section. Everything before it is pre-history: the pitch, not the work. |\n\nNo run has been scuttled yet.\nEOF\necho \"written.\" && wc -l < \"The Ship/_EJECT BUTTON/README.md\" && echo \"lines\"",

}

### OPERATOR (meta) · 2026-08-18 12:14:57 +0200

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\alder\Desktop\Factor-AI\.claude\skills\science

# Science

Capture a lesson while it is still in front of you.

The pumpjack already extracts every session transcript automatically, so this
skill is not for capture-in-general. It exists because **reasoning is not
retained in the crude** — Claude Code stores thinking blocks with their text
stripped, so transcripts hold what was said and done, never what was thought.
Anything that existed only as reasoning is gone unless it is written down
deliberately.

That is the whole job: say the reasoning out loud, in a structured way, before
it evaporates.

## When to invoke

**Operator-invoked** — they say "that's a lesson", "make that a science",
"capture that", or run `/science` directly.

**Assistant-invoked** — you decide, unprompted, because a genuine lesson just
occurred. Reasonable triggers:

- An assumption you stated confidently turned out to be wrong
- A constraint of the system or tooling was discovered by hitting it
- A design decision turned on a specific reason worth preserving
- Something failed in a way that will recur

Do not invoke for routine progress, for restating something already written
down, or to be agreeable. A lesson that could not change anyone's future
behaviour is not a lesson.

## What "invoked" means

Invoked means **who decided** to use this skill — assistant or operator — not
the mechanism that ran it.

If the operator says "hey let's make that a science" and the harness matches
this skill's description and runs it, that is **operator-invoked**. The
operator decided; the harness merely dispatched.

If you run it because you judged that a lesson had occurred, that is
**assistant-invoked**, even if you announce it first.

## Procedure

1. Read `template.md` in this skill directory. That is the exact structure to
   produce — do not improvise a different one.
2. Fill in the three frontmatter fields (see below) and answer all three
   questions *inside* their fenced blocks.
3. Write the result to `The Lab/Global Inbox/`, relative to the repository
   root. That is the repo containing the working directory Claude Code was
   started in, not necessarily the working directory itself.
4. Name the file `YYYY-MM-DD_HHMMSS.md` in local time. If that name already
   exists, append `-2`, `-3`, and so on. Seconds precision plus the collision
   check is what keeps parallel sessions from clobbering each other.
5. Tell the operator you filed it, and where.

Do not sort, categorise, tier, or colour the entry. Intake structure will be
derived later from a real corpus rather than guessed at now — templating ahead
of the data is exactly the failure this project is trying to avoid.

## The frontmatter fields

**`working_directory`** — the absolute path Claude Code was started in for this
session. Not the repository root, and not wherever the file happens to be
written. This repo is a set of sibling working directories on one machine, so
which one a lesson came from is part of the lesson. The pumpjack records this
same value in every transcript header, so the two agree and can be joined.

**`session_id`** — what makes an entry traceable back to source. Given an
entry, the readable transcript is
`Pumpjack/transcripts/<date>_<time>_<first 8 chars of session_id>.md` and the
unedited original is the matching file under `Pumpjack/crude/`. Routing in this
factory is declarative, so an item has to carry its own history — nothing about
the path it took records where it came from.

**`captured`** — local time, ISO 8601 with offset, e.g.
`2026-08-17T21:14:03+02:00`.

**Three questions, for now.** More get added when a real corpus shows what is
missing.


ARGUMENTS: Lesson: a tool's storage model constrains the ontology you can express in it, and you discover that by attempting it, not by designing. The operator proposed a run-boundary tag scheme that was conceptually correct — RUN_002 as a grouping concept, with SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY and BITER_ATTACK!! marking the run's start and end — and git rejected it in three independent ways, each of which taught something. (1) Spaces are illegal in git ref names: `fatal: 'BITER ATTACK!!' is not a valid tag name`, because refs are literally paths on disk under .git/refs/. Only spaces had to go; ` \!! ` was legal all along. (2) RUN_002 could not be both a tag and a namespace prefix: `fatal: cannot lock ref 'refs/tags/RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!': 'refs/tags/RUN_002' exists` — a ref is a file or a directory, never both, so the solo grouping tag and its children are mutually exclusive. (3) Most fundamentally, a tag pins one commit and cannot span a range, so "RUN_002 solo = the whole second run" was not expressible as a tag at all; a run is the span between two pins and git's A..B range syntax is the correct expression. Also found: tags sort alphabetically, so RUN_10 sorted before RUN_3 until zero-padded to three digits; and annotated tags record the tagging date, not the date of the commit they point at, so %(*committerdate) is the field that tells the truth about when a run actually ended. Derived rule adopted: put in a name only what git cannot tell you — git already stores both dates, but the run number exists nowhere else. Method note: every one of these was surfaced by running the command and reading the real error rather than reasoning about it in advance, and each failure improved the design rather than merely blocking it — the file/directory conflict is what forced the namespace-prefix scheme, which in turn resolved a separate open ambiguity about whether a run number referred to the run that died or the run that was born.

This project resets its world many times on purpose, and the\nrequirement was that those reset cycles be legible to someone browsing the\nrepository afterwards — that a reader could see a run's progress and then\nsee the file tree mostly deleted, with The Ship surviving as the durable\nledger. Git tags were chosen as the mechanism: immutable named coordinates,\npushed to GitHub, browsable as snapshots. The operator then designed the\nnaming scheme, in the project's own diegetic voice: BITER ATTACK!! for the\ndestruction that ends a run, SAME SHIP DIFFERENT DAY for the landing that\nbegins the next one, PROJECT_START as a one-time origin marker, and RUN_002\nas a grouping concept meaning \"the whole second run\".\n\nGit rejected this in three independent ways, each attempted and each\nproducing a real error rather than an anticipated one.\n\nFirst, spaces. `git tag -a 'BITER ATTACK!!'` returns `fatal: 'BITER\nATTACK!!' is not a valid tag name` with exit code 128. The reason is that\nrefs are literally files on disk under `.git/refs/`, so ref names inherit\npath constraints — space, tilde, caret, colon, question mark, asterisk,\nbracket and backslash are all forbidden. Notably `!!` was legal the whole\ntime and survived into the final scheme unchanged. The instinct to assume\nthe exclamation marks were the problem would have been wrong, and testing\nrather than assuming is what preserved them.\n\nSecond, uniqueness and the file/directory conflict. Tag names must be unique\nper repository, so a bare `BITER_ATTACK!!` could only ever mark one reset —\n`fatal: tag 'BITER_ATTACK!!' already exists` on the second attempt. That\nalone forces a discriminator into every name. But the deeper failure came\nwhen trying to use RUN_002 both as a standalone tag and as a namespace\nprefix: `fatal: cannot lock ref 'refs/tags/RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!':\n'refs/tags/RUN_002' exists; cannot create 'refs/tags/RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!!'`.\nBecause a ref is a path, RUN_002 can be a file or a directory and never\nboth. This was the\nuseful failure. A run genuinely is a range, git has first-class range syntax\nfor it (A..B), and the correct model was therefore two pins per boundary\nwith the run being the span between them — not one tag trying to denote an\ninterval.\n\nTwo smaller findings came out of the same testing. Tags sort\nlexicographically, so RUN_10 sorted before RUN_3 in a bare `git tag -l`\nlisting; zero-padding to three digits fixes this everywhere, including\nGitHub's tag page, whereas the `--sort=version:refname` flag only works when\nsomeone remembers to type it. The file/directory conflict forced the namespace-prefix scheme\n(RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!! rather than a solo RUN_002), and that scheme then\nresolved a completely separate open question that had been argued about\nearlier in the session on its own terms: whether the number in a boundary tag\nreferred to the run that died or the run that was born. Under the prefix\nscheme the ambiguity simply cannot arise, because the namespace carries the\nownership — RUN_002/BITER_ATTACK!! unambiguously means the death of run 2 and\nRUN_003/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY unambiguously means the birth of run 3. A\nmechanical constraint from the storage layer settled a semantic argument that\nhad no obvious resolution when treated as a naming preference.\n\nThe method note is the transferable part. Every one of these was surfaced by\nrunning the command and reading the real error, not by reasoning about git's\nrules beforehand. Reasoning in advance would have caught the spaces and\nprobably the uniqueness, but would very likely have missed the file/directory\nconflict and would have had no reason to discover that the resolution of that\nconflict also resolved the numbering ambiguity. ### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 12:16:39 +0200

Manifest complete. Seven of the ten folders are pure scaffolding — `.gitkeep` and nothing else:

```
Assemblers  Blueprint Book  Pipes  Refinery  Roboport  Smelters  Splitter
```

`The Lab` is now empty (all four entries moved). `Miners` has two real files. Those nine are clean deletions.

**`Pumpjack` is not.** It holds the only working code in the repo and the source of truth for what we just moved:

```
Pumpjack/pumpjack.py                    ← the extraction tool, 254 lines
Pumpjack/crude/*.jsonl                  ← 3 raw sessions + 4 tool-result files
Pumpjack/transcripts/                   ← now empty, we moved the renders out
```

## Two things break if it goes

**1. We'd keep the renders and delete the originals.** The `.md` transcripts now in `Unverified Inbox` are *rendered* from the `.jsonl` crude. World Design §4 calls that crude/render split "the strongest design decision in the session 002 log" and builds the whole git-is-store-of-record/MCP-is-index argument on top of it. Deleting crude inverts it — the derived copy survives, the source doesn't.

**2. Every science entry's traceability contract dies.** The skill states it plainly: given an entry, the transcript is `Pumpjack/transcripts/<date>_<time>_<first 8 of session_id>.md` and the unedited original is under `Pumpjack/crude/`. That's the *stated reason* `session_id` is a frontmatter field. All five entries — including the one I filed four minutes ago — carry session IDs that would point at nothing.

Also worth knowing: the science skill writes to `The Lab/Global Inbox/`. Delete The Lab and the next `/science` either fails or silently recreates it, resurrecting the folder.

**The mitigating fact:** every file is tracked. Nothing is destroyed — it all lives in git history permanently and is recoverable by path at any commit. This is removal from the working tree, not destruction.

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 12:20:56 +0200

Moved. One thing worth knowing about it:

```python
HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
CRUDE_DIR = HERE / "crude"
TRANSCRIPT_DIR = HERE / "transcripts"
```

**It resolves paths relative to itself**, not to the repo root. No hardcoded absolute paths — so it still runs, but from its new home it would create `_THE SHIP/Fireproof Safe/Ancient Artifacts/crude/` and `.../transcripts/` next to itself.

So right now the tool and its output are separated:

```
Pumpjack/                        ← 7 crude files + README.md, no tool
_THE SHIP/.../Ancient Artifacts/ ← the tool, no crude
```

Also stale: pumpjack.py's header says *"The refinery reads transcripts/"* — Refinery was deleted twenty minutes ago.

Two coherent end states, and I'd rather you pick than guess:

- **Retired.** pumpjack.py is an artifact now, nothing extracts going forward, and `Pumpjack/crude/` stays at root as the historical record of the three sessions that were captured. Root keeps a folder whose only job is to hold old data.
- **Fully relocated.** Move `crude/` and `README.md` in alongside the tool, then delete `Pumpjack/` entirely. Root becomes `_THE SHIP/` plus the four root files — the clean state you were driving at. The tool still works if you ever run it, and it writes next to its own crude.

The second is what your last three instructions have been pointing at, but it does mean the extraction pipeline lives in a folder named Ancient Artifacts, which reads as archived rather than live. Say which and I'll finish it.

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 12:27:48 +0200

## Proposed frontmatter

Your three required fields, plus five I'd argue for. Nothing written yet — this is a proposal.

```yaml
---
run: 001
working_directory: C:\Users\alder\Desktop\Factor-AI
session_uuid: 09f8d4b0-5de6-41ad-b2f5-a3583d054bf9
claude_session: session_01LQFpG351mjguJFPq8pqPJA
started: 2026-08-18T11:42:03+02:00
ended:   2026-08-18T13:07:55+02:00
commits:
  - sha: a49124f
    subject: The Ship never resets, and eleven dead ideas get a section of their own
  - sha: f731e2c
    subject: "V2 world design: the economy stops being decoration and starts having a bill"
tags_planted:
  - RUN_001/PROJECT_START
science_filed:
  - 2026-08-18_121514.md
---
```

| Field | Why it earns its place | Derivable? |
|---|---|---|
| `run` | Yours | **Yes** — highest `RUN_NNN` prefix with a start tag but no `BITER_ATTACK!!` |
| `working_directory` | Yours. Deliberately the same key name science entries use, so the two join | Yes, from the JSONL |
| `commits` | Yours. `sha` + `subject` so the file is readable without running git | **Yes** — `git log --grep=<claude_session>` |
| `session_uuid` | Joins to the `session_id` field every science entry already carries | Yes |
| `claude_session` | Joins to the commit trailer. **Without this, `commits` can't be verified or regenerated** — it's the only key that reaches git | Yes |
| `started` / `ended` | Ordering, duration, and a fallback way to find commits by time window if a trailer is ever missing | Yes |
| `tags_planted` | Optional. A session that planted a run boundary is the most consequential kind, and this makes it visible without diffing tags | Yes |
| `science_filed` | Optional. Links transcript → lessons without scanning the corpus | Yes |

Every field is machine-derivable — nothing here needs hand-entering, which matters given the prose ban.

## Three things this surfaces

**1. Naming collision in that folder.** It now holds two artifact types you can't distinguish by filename:

```
2026-08-17_1314_8542c6dc.md   ← transcript  (date_time_uuid8)
2026-08-17_193727.md          ← science     (date_time)
```

Frontmatter tells them apart, filenames don't. Worth a prefix or a separate folder before the corpus grows.

**2. The science template's `session_id` is now ambiguous** — there are two session identifiers and it only records one. If you want the join to work, that field should split into `session_uuid` and `claude_session` too. That's an edit to `template.md`, which changes all future entries.

**3. Nothing currently produces this.** `pumpjack.py` writes `crude/` + `transcripts/` relative to itself, emits no frontmatter of this shape, and now sits in `Ancient Artifacts/` — a folder whose name says archived. Making it emit this schema means it's live again and probably shouldn't live there.

Say yes to the schema (or cut fields) and tell me where the working extractor should live, and I'll rewrite it.

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 12:29:05 +0200

That changes the field from metadata into game state. Taking your example:

```yaml
session_cd: _THE SHIP
```

**The absolute prefix carries zero information** — `C:\Users\alder\Desktop\Factor-AI` is identical on every session on this machine. The repo-relative part is the entire signal, and under World Design §8 it's the thing that determines *which miner was running*. So this isn't a housekeeping field, it's the record of what the session was physically operating.

Two refinements I'd make to your example:

**Forward slashes, git-style.** Sessions will nest — `Miners/Miner-001-Science`, not just a top-level folder. If the value is written the way git writes paths, the field becomes directly queryable:

```bash
git log -- "$session_cd"     # what changed in that directory, ever
```

A backslash value can't do that. Windows accepts forward slashes fine.

**Repo root needs a value.** Today's session is cwd'd to the root itself. I'd write `.` — it's what git uses and it keeps the field non-empty so a parser never has to special-case it.

So the revised block:

```yaml
---
run: 001
session_cd: .                    # or: _THE SHIP, Miners/Miner-001-Science
session_uuid: 09f8d4b0-5de6-41ad-b2f5-a3583d054bf9
claude_session: session_01LQFpG351mjguJFPq8pqPJA
started: 2026-08-18T11:42:03+02:00
ended:   2026-08-18T13:07:55+02:00
commits:
  - sha: a49124f
    subject: The Ship never resets, and eleven dead ideas get a section of their own
tags_planted:
  - RUN_001/PROJECT_START
science_filed:
  - 2026-08-18_121514.md
---
```

`working_directory` is gone — `session_cd` replaces it entirely rather than sitting alongside it.

One convention call that's yours: you wrote it as `Session cd`. The existing science template uses snake_case (`working_directory`, `session_id`, `captured`), so I've written `session_cd` to match. If you'd rather the keys read as prose, say so now — it's the kind of thing that's painful to change once there's a corpus.

And the open one from before still stands: **where does the working extractor live?** It has to be rewritten to emit this, and it's currently sitting in `Ancient Artifacts/`.

Parses fine — but it caught something:

```

**YAML silently ate the zero-padding.** It read `001` as an integer. That's the exact convention we established for tag sorting an hour ago, quietly destroyed by the frontmatter format. Confirming the fix:

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 12:32:02 +0200

Quoting fixes it. So there's a rule that has to be part of the schema, not left to whoever writes the parser:

**Quote anything whose literal form matters.** Two fields hit this:
- `Run: "001"` — unquoted becomes `1`, losing the padding that makes tags sort correctly
- `Started: "2026-08-18T11:42:03+02:00"` — unquoted, YAML converts it to a datetime object and the `T` doesn't round-trip

## Final schema

```yaml
---
Run: "001"
Working Directory: _THE SHIP
Session UUID: 09f8d4b0-5de6-41ad-b2f5-a3583d054bf9
Claude Session: session_01LQFpG351mjguJFPq8pqPJA
Started: "2026-08-18T11:42:03+02:00"
Ended: "2026-08-18T13:07:55+02:00"
Commits:
  - SHA: a49124f
    Subject: "The Ship never resets, and eleven dead ideas get a section of their own"
Tags Planted:
  - RUN_001/PROJECT_START
Science Filed:
  - 2026-08-18_121514.md
---
```

`Working Directory` holds the repo-relative path — `_THE SHIP` per your example, `.` for a root session, `Miners/Miner-001-Science` when nested. Forward slashes so `git log -- "<value>"` works directly against it.

**One conflict to be aware of:** the science template already has a `working_directory` field holding an *absolute* path (`C:\Users\alder\Desktop\Factor-AI\The Ship`). Same field name, different meaning, different case. Once both artifact types sit in `Incoming Transmissions` together that's a trap. Aligning the science template to Title Case and relative paths would fix it, but it changes the shape of the four existing entries.

## Where this leaves us

Everything above is agreed but **nothing has been built** — no extractor emits this schema yet. The remaining blocker is the one I keep flagging: `pumpjack.py` has to be rewritten to produce it, and it's currently sitting in `Ancient Artifacts/`, which reads as retired.

Tell me where the working extractor lives and I'll write it. And per your earlier note — once the tree's trimmed, we commit `RUN_001/BITER_ATTACK!!`.

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 12:36:40 +0200

Critical finding: **`Claude Session` is not in the JSONL.** Both `sessionId` and `session_id` hold the same UUID; the harness ID `session_01…` appears nowhere in session storage — only in commit trailers.

That inverts one dependency: the extractor has to read commits *first* (by time window), then pull `Claude-Session:` out of them. Everything else is present — `cwd`, `timestamp`, `gitBranch`, `version`.

Reading the existing renderer so I reuse its logic rather than reinventing it:

---

}

     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""
     3	Pumpjack -- extracts crude from Claude Code session storage.
     4	
     5	Claude Code writes session transcripts as JSONL into its own storage,
     6	outside this repo, where they are subject to cleanup and invisible to
     7	git. This pulls them in.
     8	
     9	Two artifacts per session, on purpose:
    10	
    11	  crude/<stamp>_<short>.jsonl   verbatim copy, byte for byte. Never edited.
    12	  transcripts/<stamp>_<short>.md  readable, attributed rendering.
    13	
    14	The refinery reads transcripts/. crude/ exists so that a bad render is
    15	never a data loss -- you can always re-render. Refining at the pumpjack
    16	would defeat the point of having a refinery.
    17	
    18	Usage:
    19	    python pumpjack.py --project <encoded-project-dir> --date YYYY-MM-DD
    20	    python pumpjack.py --project C--Users-alder-Desktop-Factor-AI-The-Ship --date 2026-08-17
    21	"""
    22	
    23	import argparse
    24	import json
    25	import shutil
    26	from datetime import datetime, timezone
    27	from pathlib import Path
    28	
    29	# Tool results can be enormous (whole-file reads). The render truncates
    30	# them; crude/ keeps every byte.
    31	TOOL_RESULT_LIMIT = 2000
    32	TOOL_INPUT_LIMIT = 1500
    33	
    34	HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
    35	CRUDE_DIR = HERE / "crude"
    36	TRANSCRIPT_DIR = HERE / "transcripts"
    37	
    38	
    39	def local_stamp(iso_ts):
    40	    """ISO timestamp -> local-time (YYYY-MM-DD, HHMM, pretty) triple."""
    41	    dt = datetime.fromisoformat(iso_ts.replace("Z", "+00:00")).astimezone()
    42	    return dt.strftime("%Y-%m-%d"), dt.strftime("%H%M"), dt.strftime("%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S %z")
    43	
    44	
    45	def clip(text, limit):
    46	    text = text if isinstance(text, str) else json.dumps(text, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
    47	    if len(text) <= limit:
    48	        return text, False
    49	    return text[:limit], True
    50	
    51	
    52	def render_blocks(blocks, out):
    53	    """Render a content list from an assistant or user message."""
    54	    for b in blocks:
    55	        btype = b.get("type")
    56	
    57	        if btype == "text":
    58	            out.append(b.get("text", "").rstrip())
    59	            out.append("")
    60	
    61	        elif btype == "thinking":
    62	            # Claude Code's stored JSONL keeps the thinking block's signature
    63	            # but not its text -- reasoning is not retained in crude. Emitting
    64	            # an empty callout per block would be pure noise, so the count
    65	            # goes in the session header instead.
    66	            text = b.get("thinking", "").strip()
    67	            if not text:
    68	                continue
    69	            out.append("> [!note]- Thinking")
    70	            for line in text.split("\n"):
    71	                out.append("> " + line)
    72	            out.append("")
    73	
    74	        elif btype == "tool_use":
    75	            body, trimmed = clip(b.get("input", {}), TOOL_INPUT_LIMIT)
    76	            out.append(f"**→ tool call: `{b.get('name', '?')}`**")
    77	            out.append("")
    78	            out.append("```json")
    79	            out.append(body)
    80	            if trimmed:
    81	                out.append("... [truncated in render -- full input in crude/]")
    82	            out.append("```")
    83	            out.append("")
    84	
    85	        elif btype == "tool_result":
    86	            content = b.get("content", "")
    87	            if isinstance(content, list):
    88	                content = "\n".join(
    89	                    c.get("text", "") if isinstance(c, dict) else str(c) for c in content
    90	                )
    91	            body, trimmed = clip(content, TOOL_RESULT_LIMIT)
    92	            flag = " (error)" if b.get("is_error") else ""
    93	            out.append(f"**← tool result{flag}**")
    94	            out.append("")
    95	            out.append("```")
    96	            out.append(body.rstrip())
    97	            if trimmed:
    98	                out.append("... [truncated in render -- full result in crude/]")
    99	            out.append("```")
   100	            out.append("")
   101	
   102	        elif btype == "image":
   103	            out.append("*[image omitted from render -- present in crude/]*")
   104	            out.append("")
   105	
   106	
   107	def render_session(records, session_id, source_path, crude_name, sidecar_count=0, base=""):
   108	    """Turn a session's records into an attributed markdown transcript."""
   109	    turns = [r for r in records if r.get("type") in ("user", "assistant")]
   110	    if not turns:
   111	        return None, None
   112	
   113	    stamped = [r for r in records if r.get("timestamp")]
   114	    first_ts = stamped[0]["timestamp"]
   115	    last_ts = stamped[-1]["timestamp"]
   116	    date_str, time_str, first_pretty = local_stamp(first_ts)
   117	    _, _, last_pretty = local_stamp(last_ts)
   118	
   119	    meta = next((r for r in turns if r.get("cwd")), {})
   120	    models = sorted({
   121	        r["message"].get("model")
   122	        for r in turns
   123	        if r.get("type") == "assistant" and r.get("message", {}).get("model")
   124	    })
   125	
   126	    out = []
   127	    out.append(f"# Session {session_id[:8]} — {date_str}")
   128	    out.append("")
   129	    out.append("Crude extracted by the pumpjack. Attributed rendering; not edited for content.")
   130	    out.append("")
   131	    out.append("| | |")
   132	    out.append("|---|---|")
   133	    out.append(f"| Session ID | `{session_id}` |")
   134	    out.append(f"| Started | {first_pretty} |")
   135	    out.append(f"| Last activity | {last_pretty} |")
   136	    out.append(f"| Working directory | `{meta.get('cwd', '?')}` |")
   137	    out.append(f"| Git branch | `{meta.get('gitBranch', '?')}` |")
   138	    out.append(f"| Claude Code version | {meta.get('version', '?')} |")
   139	    out.append(f"| Model(s) | {', '.join(models) if models else '?'} |")
   140	    out.append(f"| Turns | {len(turns)} |")
   141	    thinking_total = sum(
   142	        1
   143	        for r in turns
   144	        if isinstance(r.get("message", {}).get("content"), list)
   145	        for b in r["message"]["content"]
   146	        if isinstance(b, dict) and b.get("type") == "thinking"
   147	    )
   148	    thinking_kept = sum(
   149	        1
   150	        for r in turns
   151	        if isinstance(r.get("message", {}).get("content"), list)
   152	        for b in r["message"]["content"]
   153	        if isinstance(b, dict) and b.get("type") == "thinking" and b.get("thinking", "").strip()
   154	    )
   155	    if thinking_total:
   156	        out.append(
   157	            f"| Thinking blocks | {thinking_total} "
   158	            f"({thinking_kept} with retained text — Claude Code strips the rest) |"
   159	        )
   160	    out.append(f"| Source | `{source_path}` |")
   161	    out.append(f"| Crude | `crude/{crude_name}` |")
   162	    if sidecar_count:
   163	        out.append(
   164	            f"| Spilled tool results | {sidecar_count} file(s) in "
   165	            f"`crude/{base}_tool-results/` — not present in the JSONL |"
   166	        )
   167	    out.append("")
   168	    out.append("---")
   169	    out.append("")
   170	
   171	    for r in turns:
   172	        role = r.get("type")
   173	        msg = r.get("message", {})
   174	        _, _, pretty = local_stamp(r["timestamp"]) if r.get("timestamp") else ("", "", "?")
   175	
   176	        if role == "user":
   177	            label = "OPERATOR"
   178	            if r.get("isMeta"):
   179	                label = "OPERATOR (meta)"
   180	        else:
   181	            label = f"CLAUDE ({msg.get('model', '?')})"
   182	
   183	        out.append(f"### {label} · {pretty}")
   184	        out.append("")
   185	
   186	        content = msg.get("content")
   187	        if isinstance(content, str):
   188	            out.append(content.rstrip())
   189	            out.append("")
   190	        elif isinstance(content, list):
   191	            render_blocks(content, out)
   192	
   193	        out.append("---")
   194	        out.append("")
   195	
   196	    return f"{date_str}_{time_str}_{session_id[:8]}", "\n".join(out)
   197	
   198	
   199	def main():
   200	    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
   201	    ap.add_argument("--project", required=True, help="Encoded project dir name under ~/.claude/projects/")
   202	    ap.add_argument("--date", required=True, help="Local date to extract, YYYY-MM-DD")
   203	    args = ap.parse_args()
   204	
   205	    src_dir = Path.home() / ".claude" / "projects" / args.project
   206	    if not src_dir.is_dir():
   207	        raise SystemExit(f"No such project storage: {src_dir}")
   208	
   209	    CRUDE_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   210	    TRANSCRIPT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   211	
   212	    extracted = []
   213	    for jsonl in sorted(src_dir.glob("*.jsonl")):
   214	        records = []
   215	        for line in jsonl.read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace").splitlines():
   216	            line = line.strip()
   217	            if not line:
   218	                continue
   219	            try:
   220	                records.append(json.loads(line))
   221	            except json.JSONDecodeError:
   222	                continue
   223	
   224	        stamped = [r for r in records if r.get("timestamp")]
   225	        if not stamped:
   226	            continue
   227	        session_date, _, _ = local_stamp(stamped[0]["timestamp"])
   228	        if session_date != args.date:
   229	            continue
   230	
   231	        session_id = jsonl.stem
   232	        base = None
   233	        # crude first, so the render can cite it
   234	        probe = [r for r in records if r.get("type") in ("user", "assistant")]
   235	        if not probe:
   236	            continue
   237	        d, t, _ = local_stamp(stamped[0]["timestamp"])
   238	        base = f"{d}_{t}_{session_id[:8]}"
   239	        crude_name = f"{base}.jsonl"
   240	
   241	        shutil.copy2(jsonl, CRUDE_DIR / crude_name)
   242	
   243	        # Large tool results do not live in the JSONL. Claude Code spills them
   244	        # to <session-id>/tool-results/toolu_*.txt and the JSONL does not even
   245	        # reference the path -- the files are keyed by tool_use id and found by
   246	        # convention. Copying only the JSONL silently loses them, and the
   247	        # overflow threshold means a naive extractor works until it doesn't.
   248	        sidecar_src = src_dir / session_id / "tool-results"
   249	        sidecar_count = 0
   250	        if sidecar_src.is_dir():
   251	            sidecar_dst = CRUDE_DIR / f"{base}_tool-results"
   252	            if sidecar_dst.exists():
   253	                shutil.rmtree(sidecar_dst)
   254	            shutil.copytree(sidecar_src, sidecar_dst)
   255	            sidecar_count = sum(1 for _ in sidecar_dst.iterdir())
   256	
   257	        name, md = render_session(records, session_id, str(jsonl), crude_name, sidecar_count, base)
   258	        if md is None:
   259	            continue
   260	        (TRANSCRIPT_DIR / f"{name}.md").write_text(md, encoding="utf-8")
   261	
   262	        extracted.append(
   263	            (name, len(probe), (CRUDE_DIR / crude_name).stat().st_size, sidecar_count)
   264	        )
   265	
   266	    if not extracted:
   267	        print(f"No sessions found for {args.date} in {args.project}")
   268	        return
   269	
   270	    print(f"Extracted {len(extracted)} session(s) for {args.date}:\n")
   271	    for name, turns, size, sidecars in extracted:
   272	        extra = f", +{sidecars} spilled tool-result file(s)" if sidecars else ""
   273	        print(f"  {name}  -  {turns} turns, {size:,} bytes crude{extra}")
   274	
   275	
   276	if __name__ == "__main__":
   277	    main()
```

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 12:37:24 +0200

---

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 12:37:26 +0200

Read it. One consequence of dropping the JSONL that needs stating plainly:

**Large tool results don't live in the JSONL.** Claude Code spills them to sidecar `.txt` files that the JSONL doesn't even reference. The old renderer truncated at 2000 chars and said *"full result in crude/"*. With no crude, that truncation becomes permanent data loss. So the new renderer has to be lossless — I'm inlining the sidecars and removing the caps.

Writing it:

---

**→ tool call: `Bash`**

```json
{
  "command": "cd \"C:/Users/alder/Desktop/Factor-AI\" && cat > \"_THE SHIP/Ignition Codes/pumpjack.py\" <<'PYEOF'\n#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\nPumpjack -- extracts session transcripts from Claude Code storage into\n_THE SHIP/SatNav/Incoming Transmissions/.\n\nClaude Code writes sessions as JSONL into its own storage, outside this\nrepo, where they are subject to cleanup and invisible to git. So this\n  renderer does not truncate, and appends every sidecar file verbatim.\n\nReasoning is still unrecoverable. Claude Code stores thinking blocks with\ntheir signature but strips the text. The header counts them; nothing can\nbring them back. That absence is why /science exists.\n\nUsage:\n    python pumpjack.py                      # today, this repo\n    python pumpjack.py --date 2026-08-17\n    python pumpjack.py --session 09f8d4b0-5de6-41ad-b2f5-a3583d054bf9\n    python pumpjack.py --all\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport re\nimport subprocess\nfrom datetime import datetime\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nHERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent\n\n# _THE SHIP/Ignition Codes/ -> repo root. Asked of git rather than assumed,\n# so moving this file one level does not silently write to the wrong tree.\nREPO_ROOT = Path(\n    subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", \"rev-parse\", \"--show-toplevel\"],\n        cwd=HERE, capture_output=True, text=True, check=True,\n    ).stdout.strip()\n)\n\nOUT_DIR = REPO_ROOT / \"_THE SHIP\" / \"SatNav\" / \"Incoming Transmissions\"\n\n\ndef git(*args, cwd=None):\n    r = subprocess.run(\n        [\"git\", *args], cwd=cwd or REPO_ROOT,\n        capture_output=True, text=True,\n    )\n    return r.stdout.strip() if r.returncode == 0 else \"\"\n\n\ndef encode_project(path: Path) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Absolute path -> Claude Code's encoded project-storage directory name.\"\"\"\n    return re.sub(r\"[:\\\\/.]\", \"-\", str(path))\n\n\ndef local(iso_ts):\n    \"\"\"ISO-8601 (UTC or offset) -> local-time datetime.\"\"\"\n    return datetime.fromisoformat(iso_ts.replace(\"Z\", \"+00:00\")).astimezone()\n\n\ndef current_run() -> str:\n    \"\"\"\n    Derive the run number from tags rather than storing it anywhere.\n\n    A run is open until its BITER_ATTACK tag exists. So: take the highest\n    RUN_NNN namespace present, and if that run has already been scuttled,\n    we are in the next one.\n    \"\"\"\n    tags = git(\"tag\", \"-l\", \"RUN_*\").splitlines()\n    runs = sorted({t.split(\"/\")[0] for t in tags if \"/\" in t})\n    if not runs:\n        return \"001\"\n    top = runs[-1]\n    n = int(top.split(\"_\")[1])\n    scuttled = any(t.startswith(f\"{top}/BITER_ATTACK\") for t in tags)\n    return f\"{n + 1:03d}\" if scuttled else f\"{n:03d}\"\n\n\ndef commits_in_window(start, end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Commits authored inside the session window.\n\n    Sessions cannot be joined to commits directly: the JSONL knows only the\n    UUID, while the commit trailer carries the harness id (session_01...).\n    Nothing records both, so the window is the only available join and it is\n    ambiguous whenever two sessions overlap. That ambiguity is reported\n    rather than hidden -- see build_frontmatter.\n    \"\"\"\n    raw = git(\n        \"log\", \"--all\",\n        f\"--since={start.isoformat()}\", f\"--until={end.isoformat()}\",\n        \"--format=%h%x1f%s%x1f%b%x1e\",\n    )\n    out = []\n    for rec in raw.split(\"\\x1e\"):\n        rec = rec.strip(\"\\n\")\n        if not rec:\n            continue\n        parts = rec.split(\"\\x1f\")\n        if len(parts) < 3:\n            continue\n        sha, subject, body = parts[0], parts[1], parts[2]\n        m = re.search(r\"^Claude-Session:\\s*\\S*?(session_\\w+)\\s*$\", body, re.M)\n        out.append({\"sha\": sha, \"subject\": subject, \"trailer\": m.group(1) if m else None})\n    return out\n\n\ndef tags_in_window(start, end):\n    raw = git(\"for-each-ref\", \"--format=%(refname:short)%09%(creatordate:iso-strict)\", \"refs/tags\")\n    found = []\n    for line in raw.splitlines():\n        if \"\\t\" not in line:\n            continue\n        name, when = line.split(\"\\t\", 1)\n        try:\n            ts = datetime.fromisoformat(when).astimezone()\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        if start <= ts <= end:\n            found.append(name)\n    return sorted(found)\n\n\ndef science_in_window(session_uuid):\n    \"\"\"Science entries filed by this session, matched on the id they record.\"\"\"\n    if not OUT_DIR.is_dir():\n        return []\n    hits = []\n    for f in sorted(OUT_DIR.glob(\"*.md\")):\n        head = f.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", out.append(\"### `\" + p.name + \"`\")\n                out.append(\"\")\n                out.append(\"```\")\n                out.append(p.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").rstrip())\n                out.append(\"```\")\n                out.append(\"\")\n\n    name = start.strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d_%H%M\") + \"_\" + session_uuid[:8]\n    return name, \"\\n\".join(out)\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"--project\", help=\"Encoded dir under ~/.claude/projects/ (default: this repo)\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--date\", help=\"Local date to extract, YYYY-MM-DD (default: today)\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--session\", help=\"Extract one session UUID\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--all\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Extract every session in storage\")\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    project = args.project or encode_project(REPO_ROOT)\n    src_dir = Path.home() / \".claude\" / \"projects\" / project\n    if not src_dir.is_dir():\n        raise SystemExit(\"No such project storage: \" + str(src_dir))\n\n    want_date = None\n    if not args.all and not args.session:\n        want_date = args.date or datetime.now().strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d\")\n\n    OUT_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n\n    extracted = []\n    for jsonl in sorted(src_dir.glob(\"*.jsonl\")):\n        session_uuid = jsonl.stem\n        if args.session and session_uuid != args.session:\n            continue\n\n        records = []\n        for line in jsonl.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\").splitlines():\n            line = line.strip()\n            if not line:\n                continue\n            try:\n                records.append(json.loads(line))\n            except json.JSONDecodeError:\n                continue\n\n        stamped = [r for r in records if r.get(\"timestamp\")]\n        if not stamped:\n            continue\n        if want_date and local(stamped[0][\"timestamp\"]).strftime(\"%Y-%m-%d\") != want_date:\n            continue\n\n        sidecar = src_dir / session_uuid / \"tool-results\"\n        name, md = render_session(records, session_uuid, str(jsonl), sidecar)\n        if md is None:\n            continue\n\n        dest = OUT_DIR / (name + \".md\")\n        dest.write_text(md, encoding=\"utf-8\")\n        extracted.append((dest.name, len(md)))\n\n    if not extracted:\n        target = args.session or want_date or \"all sessions\"\n        print(\"Nothing extracted for \" + str(target) + \" in \" + project)\n        return\n\n    print(\"Extracted {} session(s) to {}:\\n\".format(len(extracted), OUT_DIR))\n    for name, size in extracted:\n        print(\"  {}  -  {:,} bytes\".format(name, size))\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

     1	---
     2	name: science
     3	description: Capture a key lesson from the current session as a structured entry in The Lab's Global Inbox. Use when the operator says something is a lesson, a learning, or "make that a science" — and use it on your own initiative when a genuine lesson has just occurred (a wrong assumption corrected, a constraint discovered, a design decision that turned on a specific reason). Records who invoked it, why, and the lesson in full self-contained detail.
     4	---
     5	
     6	# Science
     7	
     8	Capture a lesson while it is still in front of you.
     9	
    10	The pumpjack already extracts every session transcript automatically, so this
    11	skill is not for capture-in-general. It exists because **reasoning is not
    12	retained in the crude** — Claude Code stores thinking blocks with their text
    13	stripped, so transcripts hold what was said and done, never what was thought.
    14	Anything that existed only as reasoning is gone unless it is written down
    15	deliberately.
    16	
    17	That is the whole job: say the reasoning out loud, in a structured way, before
    18	it evaporates.
    19	
    20	## When to invoke
    21	
    22	**Operator-invoked** — they say "that's a lesson", "make that a science",
    23	"capture that", or run `/science` directly.
    24	
    25	**Assistant-invoked** — you decide, unprompted, because a genuine lesson just
    26	occurred. Reasonable triggers:
    27	
    28	- An assumption you stated confidently turned out to be wrong
    29	- A constraint of the system or tooling was discovered by hitting it
    30	- A design decision turned on a specific reason worth preserving
    31	- Something failed in a way that will recur
    32	
    33	Do not invoke for routine progress, for restating something already written
    34	down, or to be agreeable. A lesson that could not change anyone's future
    35	behaviour is not a lesson.
    36	
    37	## What "invoked" means
    38	
    39	Invoked means **who decided** to use this skill — assistant or operator — not
    40	the mechanism that ran it.
    41	
    42	If the operator says "hey let's make that a science" and the harness matches
    43	this skill's description and runs it, that is **operator-invoked**. The
    44	operator decided; the harness merely dispatched.
    45	
    46	If you run it because you judged that a lesson had occurred, that is
    47	**assistant-invoked**, even if you announce it first.
    48	
    49	## Procedure
    50	
    51	1. Read `template.md` in this skill directory. That is the exact structure to
    52	   produce — do not improvise a different one.
    53	2. Fill in the three frontmatter fields (see below) and answer all three
    54	   questions *inside* their fenced blocks.
    55	3. Write the result to `The Lab/Global Inbox/`, relative to the repository
    56	   root. That is the repo containing the working directory Claude Code was
    57	   started in, not necessarily the working directory itself.
    58	4. Name the file `YYYY-MM-DD_HHMMSS.md` in local time. If that name already
    59	   exists, append `-2`, `-3`, and so on. Seconds precision plus the collision
    60	   check is what keeps parallel sessions from clobbering each other.
    61	5. Tell the operator you filed it, and where.
    62	
    63	Do not sort, categorise, tier, or colour the entry. Intake structure will be
    64	derived later from a real corpus rather than guessed at now — templating ahead
    65	of the data is exactly the failure this project is trying to avoid.
    66	
    67	## The frontmatter fields
    68	
    69	**`working_directory`** — the absolute path Claude Code was started in for this
    70	session. Not the repository root, and not wherever the file happens to be
    71	written. This repo is a set of sibling working directories on one machine, so
    72	which one a lesson came from is part of the lesson. The pumpjack records this
    73	same value in every transcript header, so the two agree and can be joined.
    74	
    75	**`session_id`** — what makes an entry traceable back to source. Given an
    76	entry, the readable transcript is
    77	`Pumpjack/transcripts/<date>_<time>_<first 8 chars of session_id>.md` and the
    78	unedited original is the matching file under `Pumpjack/crude/`. Routing in this
    79	factory is declarative, so an item has to carry its own history — nothing about
    80	the path it took records where it came from.
    81	
    82	**`captured`** — local time, ISO 8601 with offset, e.g.
    83	`2026-08-17T21:14:03+02:00`.
    84	
    85	**Three questions, for now.** More get added when a real corpus shows what is
    86	missing.
```

Repointed. Plain English:

**Four edits to `.claude/skills/science/SKILL.md`:**

1. **Description** — now says "the Ship's Incoming Transmissions."
2. **Write path** — `_THE SHIP/SatNav/Incoming Transmissions/`, with the reason attached: the Ship never resets, so entries there survive every planet.
3. **The "reasoning is not retained in the crude" premise** — crude no longer exists, so it now says reasoning is retained *nowhere*, and notes the crude never held it either. The skill's justification is actually stronger than before.
4. **`session_id` traceability** — repointed to the sibling transcript in the same folder, and honest that there's no unedited original any more, with the git-history recovery command. It also now notes the link resolves from both ends, since the pumpjack reads `session_id` back out and lists matches under `Science Filed`.

The only surviving `Pumpjack/` references are the deliberate recovery instructions.

**One thing I corrected rather than left standing.** The skill claimed *"the pumpjack records this same value in every transcript header, so the two agree and can be joined."* That's now false — pumpjack writes `Working Directory` (Title Case, repo-relative) and the skill writes `working_directory` (snake_case, absolute). I marked the divergence explicitly and pointed the join at `session_id`, which is exact. **I did not reconcile them** — you said Title Case for variable names, but changing the template alters all future entries and diverges from the five that exist. That's your call, flagged in the file as an open decision.

**Worth noting:** you can see in this transcript that the skill's description updated *live* — the harness re-read it mid-session after each edit. That's a partial data point against session 003's live-pickup failure, though not a resolution: it shows an already-loaded skill's edits propagate, not that a newly-created skill in a parent directory gets discovered. The §16 test is still unrun.

**Open, and I'd suggest this order:**

1. Re-run `pumpjack.py` at session end — today's transcript is partial.
2. Commit `RUN_001/BITER_ATTACK!!` and push tags.
3. Filenames still don't distinguish transcripts from science entries in that folder.
4. Whether the science template moves to Title Case.

All ledger queries work. Plain English:

**Link sweep**

Found no markdown or wikilink syntax anywhere — every reference in this repo is a bare path in prose, which is why nothing was catching them. Fixed four broken ones:

- Root README: `The Ship/Distress Beacon/...` → `_THE SHIP/...`
- Root README: `/_THE TUTORIAL/` → `/_THE SHIP/Hitchhiker's Guide/`, plus `YAML Schema/` → `Template Schema/` (the folder was renamed) and a pointer to a deleted `Science Ore Patch_v1` → the surviving copy in Ancient Artifacts
- `_EJECT BUTTON/README.md`: the invariant said `The Ship/` — I'd written it before the rename
- `World Design.md`: a live pointer at `The Lab/Global Inbox/…`

I also added two lines to the README pointing at SatNav and the Eject Button, since neither was mentioned anywhere.

**Deliberately not fixed:** the Distress Beacon logs. They're dated records of past state — rewriting them would falsify the account. Same for the archived `Science Ore Patch.md`, which references directories that existed when it was written. The `Lexicon.md` entries for Refinery, Roboport, Smelters etc. still define machines whose directories are gone; those are ontology terms, not links, and you're editing that file right now anyway.

**Two commits, per the ritual**

```
2121230  BITER ATTACK: the planet is scuttled, the Ship carries on      16 files, deletions only
9f505aa  The Ship becomes _THE SHIP, and the run ledger gets a…         67 files, +27,104

The invariant held — the scuttle touches **zero** files under `_THE SHIP/`.

**I made a mistake mid-way.** When moving a stray file into the first commit I used `--amend` while the deletions were still staged, so commit 1 swallowed 14 of them. Caught it in the verification step, rewound to the pre-amend commit via reflog, and rebuilt the split properly. Nothing was lost and nothing was pushed.

**Two things you should know**

1. **Run 001 is now closed.** `pumpjack.py` derives the run from tags, so it will report `Run: "002"` from here on — but there's no `RUN_002/SAME_SHIP_DIFFERENT_DAY` yet. The ledger has an open gap until you plant one.
2. **Nothing is pushed.** Two commits and one tag are local. `git push origin main --tags` when you want it public — say the word and I'll run it.

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 13:45:00 +0200

Found it. **The Debris Protocol.**

| Session | cwd | Wrote |
|---|---|---|
| `7d4e1317` 19:16 | The Ship | **`debris-protocol.html`** ← the MCP document |
| `41d1dc4b` 20:42 | The Ship | `World Design.md` (in the repo) |
| `2bdc82b4` 23:31 | The Ship | nothing |

It's the artifact I read at the start of this session. Its Architecture section is the MCP setup discussion:

- **Why MCP at all** — *"An MCP server is the only place a world can hide from both of us. State lives behind a process, not in files — there's nothing to open, and `ls` tells you nothing."*
- **The engine/cartridge split** — you write the server, it stays content-blind, Claude writes the world data. You can't be surprised by a world you authored.
- **Two July 2026 spec changes that land on the design** — sessions are gone (`Mcp-Session-Id` removed), so the guy's identity must be a parameter on every call rather than a global; and `tools/list` no longer varies per connection, so unlock-by-tool-appearing is single-player only. *"Absence is a single-player mechanic. Denial is the multiplayer one."*
- **Despair derived, not ticked** — `floor((now − start) / 30)`, computed on read.
- **SDK maturity** — Python dominates; Python SDK v2 still beta, FastMCP renamed its main class in v4.

## The problem with it

**It has never been in the repo.** I checked history and the working tree — no trace, ever. It exists in exactly two places:

1. `…\Temp\claude\…\7d4e1317…\scratchpad\debris-protocol.html` — a **temp directory**, subject to cleanup
2. A private artifact URL on claude.ai

For a build-in-public project whose stated premise is that git is the store of record and the compiled layer is derived, the most substantive MCP design document of run 001 — the one carrying five blocking decisions — is sitting in `%TEMP%`. It also survived the biter attack purely by accident, since the scuttle couldn't delete what was never tracked.

## Also worth knowing

**Four of the seven `The Ship` sessions have never been extracted.** Only `8542c6dc`, `52fa9197` and `ce91eb3f` made it into the repo. Missing: `7d4e1317` (the MCP one, 1.1MB), `41d1dc4b`, `2bdc82b4`, `eb781bc7`. The new extractor can pull them with `--project C--Users-alder-Desktop-Factor-AI-The-Ship --all`, and they'd correctly record `Working Directory: The Ship` as it was at the time.

Want me to extract those four, bring the Debris Protocol into the repo, or both?

### CLAUDE (claude-opus-5) · 2026-08-18 13:47:11 +0200

Pulled, and the joins all resolved — `Claude Session`, both commits, and all three science entries linked automatically. But there's a bug I need to fix before this gets committed:

```yaml
Run: "002"        ← wrong. That session ran during run 001.
```

`current_run()` reads the tags *as they are now*. Run 001 is scuttled, so every historical session gets stamped with today's run. The run has to be derived from **when the session happened**, not when it was extracted:

  "old_string": "def current_run():\n    \"\"\"\n    Derive the run number from tags rather than storing it anywhere.\n\n    A run is open until its BITER_ATTACK tag exists. So: take the highest\n    RUN_NNN namespace present, and if that run has already been scuttled,\n    we are in the next one.\n    \"\"\"\n    tags = git(\"tag\", \"-l\", \"RUN_*\").splitlines()\n    runs = sorted({t.split(\"/\")[0] for t in tags if \"/\" in t})\n    if not runs:\n        return \"001\"\n    top = runs[-1]\n    try:\n        n = int(top.split(\"_\")[1])\n    except (IndexError, ValueError):\n        return \"001\"\n    scuttled = any(t.startswith(top + \"/BITER_ATTACK\") for t in tags)\n    return \"{:03d}\".format(n + 1 if scuttled else n)",

Dated by the *commit* the tag points at, not by when\n    the tag was planted -- those differ whenever a boundary is tagged late,\n    and the commit is the one that tells the truth about when the run began.\n    \"\"\"\n    raw = git(\n        \"for-each-ref\",\n        \"--format=%(refname:short)\\t%(*committerdate:iso-strict)\",\n        \"refs/tags\",\n    )\n    out = []\n    for line in raw.splitlines():\n        if \"\\t\" not in line:\n            continue\n        name, when = line.split(\"\\t\", 1)\n        if \"/\" not in name or not name.startswith(\"RUN_\"):\n            continue\n        head, leaf = name.split(\"/\", 1)\n        if not (leaf.startswith(\"PROJECT_START\") or leaf.startswith(\"SAME_SHIP\")):\n            continue\n        try:\n            out.append((int(head.split(\"_\")[1]), datetime.fromisoformat(when).astimezone()))\n        except (IndexError, ValueError):\n            continue\n    return sorted(out, key=lambda x: x[1])\n\n\ndef run_for(started):\n    \"\"\"\n    Which run was open when this session ran.\n\n    Derived from the session's own start time rather than from the current\n    tag state -- otherwise re-extracting an old session after a scuttle\n    stamps it with today's run, which is how a ledger starts lying.\n    \"\"\"\n    bounds = run_boundaries()\n    if not bounds:\n        return \"001\"\n    current = bounds[0][0]\n    for n, opened in bounds:\n        if opened <= started:\n            current = n\n        else:\n            break\n    return \"{:03d}\".format(current)"
